```
def fast_convolution_max(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n<=4096: c=np.convolve(a,a); max_c=max(c)
    else: L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2; fa=fft(a, L)[:n]?; fc=ifft(fa*fa).real[:2*n-1]; max_c=max(fc)
    S=sum(a)
    if S<0.01: inf
    return 2*n*max_c/(S*S)
```

The text: "Starting from a nonnegative step function f normalized so sum=√(2n), set M=||f*f||∞. Next compute g0=(b0,...,bn-1) by solving a linear program, i.e. maximizing ∑ b_j subject to b_j≥0 and ||f*g0||∞≤M; as is standard, the optimum is attained at an extreme point determined by an active set of binding inequalities... Rescale g0 to match normalization... update f←(1-t)f+t g. Repeating produces a sequence with nonincreasing ||f*f||∞." This is exactly a known algorithm for "N-P (nonnegative) perfect sequences". Weight optimization algorithm for fixed positions:
1. Initialize weights. Options:
   - Uniform
   - Random lognormal normalized
   - Spectral: weights inversely proportional to diagonal degree? Not.
2. Objective f(w) = max c(w) (with sum1). We can project gradient descent on the simplex.
   - Smooth objective: use softmax over top coefficients. For m small, can use exact max active set with several active bins.
   - Since c is quadratic but positive, gradient B@w where B_{i,j}=p_{pos_i+pos_j}. For active p, f_p =∑ p_k c_k. Gradient = 2 B_p w. Projected update on simplex: w <- proj_simplex(w - lr gradient). For nonconvex, need small lr and avoid negative. Since scale fixed, we can also use multiplicative updates (EM) to maintain positivity.

Multiplicative update (like for nonnegative quadratic minimization):
For objective max c, define violation v_i? If we minimize ∑ p_k c_k, gradient positive. Multiplicative: w_i ← w_i * exp(-η * gradient_i / current? ), then normalize. Or use w_i ← w_i / (1 + η ∇_i) normalized. This stays positive.
Smoothness: f is quadratic; step size related to Lipschitz: Lipschitz of gradient ≤? c_k ≤? On simplex, ∇f_p entries ≤2 maybe? Choose η ~0.05.
Can use adaptive learning rate and line search: try multiple η (e.g., 0.01..0.5), evaluate new w (sparse eval) choose best, else reduce.

Alternative: Use Newton/solve KKT with active set: At optimum, for active bins k, for all i: ∑ p_k? We can iterate λ. Maybe gradient enough.

Let's design weight local search in detail:
Input: pos array length m, n = pos.max()+1 (after shift min to0). Nbins=2*n-1. Precompute:
- `pair_sums = pos[:,None] + pos[None,:]` shape m×m (int). For m=200, 40k.
- For convolution eval from w full: `off = (w[:,None]*w[None,:]).ravel()`? This counts each ordered pair once. Then `c = np.bincount(pair_sums.ravel(), weights=off.ravel(), minlength=Nbins)`. This O(m²) and simple. For m=200, easy. For gradient, B=p[pair_sums] matrix. But for local moves, use update formula to avoid recompute.
- For local moves update, need pos_i, pos_j, and maybe all pos. Use c array length Nbins. For a move i,j,δ:
  old_max? We'll apply and revert.
  To accumulate deltas efficiently, avoid temporary full array? Affected sums are pos_i+pos and pos_j+pos (2m). We can allocate a temporary array of zeros Nbins? For m=200, Nbins~14k, allocate per move too heavy. Use a Python dict? 2m up to400, update c entries directly with care for overlaps. Since affected sums may overlap, if we just `c[s] += delta` for each entry, overlapping sums get multiple additions correctly as long as we don't need to revert? We can keep a list `(s, delta)` and apply; for overlapping, multiple entries in list; applying sequentially works. But for reverting, need revert exact deltas; can apply negative sequentially. Or record old values for touched sums to revert. Simpler: For candidate move, create a temporary `delta_dict` using numpy? Since m small, use Python list of pairs and combine via dict to ensure correct if overlapping; then apply. For each `s,delta`: if s not in touched: touched[s]=c[s]; c[s]+=delta. After evaluating, revert: for s,old in touched.items(): c[s]=old. This dict overhead per move (<=400). If many moves (100k), Python dict maybe okay but could be slow. Alternative use full copy of c for affected? Too heavy.

We can precompute `pos_i_plus = pos + pos_i` arrays for each i; `pos_j_plus`. Then for a move, affected arrays length m each. We can compute deltas:
  d_i = -2*δ*w  (array length m)
  d_j =  +2*δ*w
Need apply to c at s_i_k = pos_i+pos_k, s_j_k=pos_j+pos_k. Plus three quadratic points. For speed, we can update in place with a copy of c? We can get unaffected max by either keeping a max-heap or recompute. We can maintain a sorted structure? Given Nbins small, recompute `c.max()` after in-place update might be simplest.
Given time budget and m small, we can evaluate candidate moves by copying c. Let's design a fast local search with vectorized copy:
```
def trial_move(c, pos, w, i, j, delta):
    c2 = c.copy()
    s1 = pos_i_plus[i]
    s2 = pos_i_plus[j]
    c2[s1] -= 2*delta*w
    c2[s2] += 2*delta*w
    c2[2*pos[i]] += delta*delta
    c2[pos[i]+pos[j]] -= 2*delta*delta
    c2[2*pos[j]] += delta*delta
    maxc = c2.max()
    return maxc
```
Here `pos_i_plus[i]` is precomputed `pos + pos[i]`. For a move, we copy c (Python C) and do vector additions (2m). Then max (C). For m=200, n=10000, copy 20kb? Actually 14k floats=112KB; 10k candidates=1.1GB copy, okay. 100k=11GB, likely 5-10s. We can do ~10k candidates, enough? Local search may need more. We can optimize by only copying if the move is promising? We can sample candidate delta and check with cheaper approximate: the maximum of delta? But maybe fine.

We can reduce length n by using p=83, Nbins~13777, copy 110KB. 100k candidates =11GB; memory bandwidth 20GB/s ->0.5s plus numpy overhead per op (4 vector ops) maybe 5-10s. 1M candidates 50-100s. 2400s allows maybe 1M? But Python overhead per candidate (function calls, numpy operations) maybe ~50µs? 1M 50s? Could be. We can implement very simple loops.

Local search strategy per support:
- Normalize w to sum1.
- Compute c = conv via bincount pair_sums weights w outer.
- best_w, best_max.
- For a number of restart initializations:
   * Uniform weights
   * Random weights: w = exp(rng.normal(0,sigma)) normalized, sigma 0.5,1,2
   * Greedy: start with one support point 1, gradually add mass to points that keep max low? Not.
- Run improvement loops:
   For iter in range(max_iter_until_time or no improvement):
      Choose random i,j. Maybe bias i to high weight or high bound? If i==j skip.
      Decide direction: either move mass from i to j (if w_i > w_j) or from j to i. We can allow both by picking i donor = higher weight with prob0.8, random otherwise, recipient lower.
      For donor d, recipient r, set a = w[d]. Try fractions [0.01,0.03,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5, 0.9] times a. Also maybe move a fixed fraction of total 1/m? We can try exponentiated scaling? 
      For each δ in fractions (sorted maybe small to large) compute c2 and max. Track best δ if improves over current max; but we need ensure after best δ, subsequent candidate based on original c? We can just apply the first improvement? Better evaluate candidates on original c to avoid self contamination; choose best δ among tried. Apply it (c,w) and accept if max improves. If no single δ improves, maybe try random perturbation with small worsening (annealing) or just break to new restart.
   - Also try block moves: move mass from a group to another? Random pair may suffice.
   - Periodically run gradient-like update: compute active top q bins, p matrix, gradient, multiplicative update w=normalize(w - η gradient or w*exp(-η grad)). Evaluate and accept if improves. This can smooth.

Given m small, gradient update could be efficient and find better local minima. Let's implement both.

Gradient update for weights:

Local moves:
Precompute for each i: pos_plus_i = pos[i] + pos (array length m).
A move d (decrease) to r (increase):
```
def evaluate_move(c, w, d, r, delta):
    c2 = c.copy()
    s_d = pos_plus[d]
    s_r = pos_plus[r]
    c2[s_d] -= (2*delta)*w
    c2[s_r] += (2*delta)*w
    dd = delta*delta
    c2[2*pos[d]] += dd
    c2[pos[d]+pos[r]] -= 2*dd
    c2[2*pos[r]] += dd
    return c2, c2.max()
```
Because c array length Nbin; affected sums within 0..2n-2 since positions <n.

For a move, w_d may be zero; skip. Fractions: we can try [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.9] of w_d. Also maybe absolute small = 1/m/factor. We can scale to sum1.

Accept best delta if maxc < current_max - eps. We need update c and w if accept. We can reuse c2 from best to avoid recompute; set c=c2, w[d]-=δ, w[r]+=δ. To find best, loop deltas, store best_c (copy) and max. For each delta, if c2.max() < best_max. It allocates c2 each delta; okay.

To avoid too much local search stuck, add occasional "kick": random move with small worsening (annealing) if no improvement after many tries. Use temperature decreasing.
Pseudo:
```
best_w, best_max = ...

for step in range(max_steps):
   if no_improve>steps_per_kick:
       # random kick: choose random move δ and apply even if worsens by < temp
       ...
   choose donor/recipient; try moves; if improvement accept else if no improve after threshold, run gradient or kick.
   if cur_max<best_max: update best.
   if time>per_support_budget: break.
```

But the objective in log domain with softmax:
```
def softmax(u):
  u-=u.max(); e=np.exp(u); return e/e.sum()
def conv_w(w): ...
def smooth_loss(u, pos, pair_sums, Nbin, tau):
  w=softmax(u); c=conv; return logsumexp(tau*c)/tau
def smooth_grad(u, pos,pair_sums,Nbin,tau):
  w=softmax(u); c=conv; z=tau*c - tau*max(c); p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum(); B=p[pair_sums]; g=2*B.dot(w); grad=w*(g - np.dot(w,g)); return grad
```
But `p[pair_sums]` builds m*m array. For tau large, p concentrated. We can use tau = 1/(maxc) * 10? Since maxc ~1e-4, tau~1e5; exp(tau*c) overflow; subtract max. Good. Use maybe tau = 50/(maxc) so spread top. For `loss`, not used for final score; just guide.

Implement active gradient step:
```
def active_gradient_step(c,w,pair_sums,q,eta_list):
    # choose q top bins
    top = np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
    p = np.zeros(Nbin); p[top]=1/q
    B = p[pair_sums]
    g = 2*(B@w)
    best = None
    for eta in eta_list:
       w2 = w * np.exp(-eta*g)
       # normalize
       w2s=w2.sum(); if very small...
       w2/=w2s
       c2=conv(w2)
       max2=c2.max()
       if max2<best_max: best...
    return best
```

`conv_weights(w, pair_sums_flat, Nbin)`:
```
def conv_weights(w, pair_sums_flat, Nbin):
    m=len(w)
    outer = np.outer(w, w).ravel()
    c = np.bincount(pair_sums_flat, weights=outer, minlength=Nbin)
    c = c[:Nbin]
    c[c<0]=0
    return c.astype(np.float64, copy=False)
```
`np.bincount` returns length at least Nbin; if weights float, returns float64. Good.

Support generation:
```
def generate_support_candidates(p, rng, max_params=200):
    cands=[]
    base_a0=1
    # Precompute base positions for i
    base_pos = [(i*p + ( (1*i*i) % p )) for i in range(p)]
    param_list=[]
    for a1 in [2,3,4,5,-1,-2,-3,-4, p-1, p-2, p-3]:
       for b1 in [0,1,-1,2,-2,3,-3,4,-4]:
          for c1 in [1,2,3,4,5, p-1,p-2,p-3, (p//3), (2*p//3)]:
             param_list.append((a1%p,b1%p,c1%p))
    # maybe add random params if time
    for _ in range(80): param_list.append((rng.integers(1,p), rng.integers(p), rng.integers(1,p)))
    seen=set()
    for a,b,c in param_list:
        if (a,b,c) in seen: continue
        second_low = [ (a*i*i + b*i + c) % p for i in range(p)]
        # positions
        p0 = base_pos
        p1 = [i*p + second_low[i] for i in range(p)]
        s=set(p0); for x in p1: if x not in s: add
        pos=np.array(sorted(s))
        if len(pos) < int(1.8*p): continue? m too low maybe.
        if pos[0]!=0: pos-=pos[0]
        n=pos[-1]+1
        if n>MAX_N or n<10: continue
        # quick unweighted score
        pair_sums = (pos[:,None]+pos[None,:]).ravel()
        counts=np.bincount(pair_sums, minlength=2*n-1)
        Rmax=int(counts.max())
        m=len(pos)
        score=2.0*n*Rmax/(m*m)
        cands.append((score, Rmax, pos.copy()))
        if len(cands)>500: break
    return cands
```

Weight optimization with local moves + active gradient:
```
def optimize_weights_fixed(pos,n,w,rng,budget):
    m=len(pos); Nbin=2*n-1
    pair_flat=(pos[:,None]+pos[None,:]).ravel()
    pair_mat=pair_flat.reshape(m,m)
    pos_plus = [pos[i]+pos for i in range(m)] # list of arrays? 160*160 memory small.
    w=normalize(w)
    c=conv_weights(w,pair_flat,Nbin)
    cur_max=c.max(); best_w=w.copy(); best_max=cur_max
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    # Active gradient phase
    grad_deadline=min(deadline, time+max(1.0,budget*0.25))
    while time<grad_deadline:
       improved=False
       for q in [5,10,20,50]:
           if cur_max==0: break
           top=np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
           p=np.zeros(Nbin); p[top]=1/len(top)
           B=p[pair_mat]
           g=2*B.dot(w)
           best_cand=None
           for eta in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]:
               w2=w*np.exp(-eta*g)
               s=w2.sum()
               if s<=0 or not finite: continue
               w2/=s
               c2=conv_weights(w2,pair_flat,Nbin)
               max2=c2.max()
               if best_cand is None or max2<best_cand_max: store
           if best_cand: w,c,cur_max=...; if cur_max<best...; improved=True; break
       if not improved: break
    # Local move phase until deadline
    no_improve=0; kick_every=200; temp=cur_max*1e-3; best_no_improve...
    while time<deadline:
       i=j=...
       if w_i<=0...
       donor,recipient...
       a=w[donor]
       deltas=fractions*a
       best_cand=None
       for delta in deltas:
           # Ensure delta<long?; if delta>=a: delta=0.999*a
           c2 = move_trial(c,w,recipient,donor,delta,pos_plus,pos)
           max2=c2.max()
           if max2<best_max_cand: store c2,max2
       if best_cand:
           c=best_c; w[donor]-=δ; w[rec]+=δ; cur_max=bestmax; if <best: update; no_improve=0; temp=max(1e-12, temp*0.99)
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve%kick_every==0:
              # random kick: choose delta small, apply even if worsen but temp limit
              # maybe pick from candidate if max2 < cur_max + temp
              if best_cand_with_worsen: apply temp; if cur_max<best? no
              else: do a random small gradient? or restart weights? 
    return best_w,best_max
```
This could be time-consuming in Python loops. Number of local move trials: each trial copies c (Nbin) and vector operations. If m=160, Nbin~13k, maybe 50k trials/sec? Python maybe lower. We can set phases accordingly.

Let's implement `trial_move_cand(c, w, pos_plus, pos, donor, recipient, delta)` as a top-level function returning `(c2, maxc)` or None. To avoid function call overhead inside loops, maybe inline in `optimize_weights_fixed`? But code clarity. We can define top-level and call; okay.

`trial_move_cand`:
```
def trial_move_cand(c, w, pos_plus, pos, donor, recipient, delta):
    c2 = c.copy()
    coef = 2.0*delta
    c2[pos_plus[donor]] -= coef * w
    c2[pos_plus[recipient]] += coef * w
    d2 = delta*delta
    s_ii = 2*pos[donor]
    s_ij = pos[donor]+pos[recipient]
    s_jj = 2*pos[recipient]
    c2[s_ii] += d2
    c2[s_ij] -= 2.0*d2
    c2[s_jj] += d2
    maxc = float(c2.max())
    return c2, maxc
```
Need if donor and recipient same skip. If s_ii==s_ij etc? Donor≠recipient; s_ij may equal s_ii if pos[recipient]=pos[donor]? duplicates removed, so distinct positions. s_ij could equal s_jj? no. If positions distinct but 2pos_i=pos_i+pos_j if pos_i=pos_j no. So assignments add; if same index somehow, we need accumulate not overwrite. We use in-place additions `+=`, so if same index, it works. Good.
However, `c2[pos_plus[donor]] -= coef*w` modifies array; if donor sum array and recipient sum array overlap, order of additions matters? We are adding linear deltas; because additions commute. The diagonal corrections are separate. If an index in pos_plus[donor] equals an index in pos_plus[recipient], the two vector operations add correctly. But if pos_plus[donor] array and pos_plus[recipient] array overlap and we do `c2[indices_d] -= coef*w` then `c2[indices_r] += coef*w`, if same index and same w? The net is correct (operations are independent and commute). Good.

But there is a subtlety: `pos_plus[donor]` includes index donor (sum 2pos_d). Then diagonal correction adds d2. `pos_plus[recipient]` includes index recipient? For sum 2pos_r, coefficient +coef*w_r then +d2. Correct.

The formula with vector `w` uses old w values. Since we only compute candidate from old c,w, okay. But when `pos_plus[donor]` and `pos_plus[recipient]` arrays overlap, the order of vectorized addition/subtraction doesn't double apply coefficients? For a moved sum s that equals some pos_d+pos_k and pos_r+pos_l, the true linear delta is -2δ w_k + 2δ w_l. We add -coef*w_k at index, +coef*w_l at maybe same index. If both operations refer to same index but different positions in arrays, using advanced indexing with repeated indices? `c2[indices] -= values` performs elementwise for each element; if indices contains duplicates within the same operation, does it accumulate? In numpy, `arr[indices] -= values` with repeated indices: I think it is not guaranteed to accumulate correctly? It uses buffered? Actually `arr[idx] += val` with duplicate idx may add val only once? Let's recall: In NumPy, advanced indexing with `arr[idx] += val` when idx has repeated elements may not do multiple additions as expected due to buffering? I suspect it may add only once or copy-on-write. For `c2[indices_d] -= coef*w`, if `indices_d` has no duplicates because donor distinct from all k? pos_d + pos_k; since pos unique, sums with same donor are unique (pos_k distinct). So no duplicates within one op. Overlap between d and r arrays: operations separate, each may do one addition/subtraction to that index. If an index appears once in d and once in r, the operations are separate; `c2[idx] -= val1; c2[idx] += val2` works. Good. No duplicate within an array.

But when assigning diagonal corrections with scalar index that could be same as an advanced index, separate operations fine.

Position moves:
```
def position_moves(pos,n,w,rng,budget):
    # Build pair_flat for current pos,n each time.
    best_pos=pos.copy(); best_n=n; best_w=w.copy(); best_score=2*n*max(conv)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    while time<deadline:
       # random i, new_pos
       i=rng.integers(m)
       # radius
       rad = int(max(1, (n/20)*rng.random()*10?))
       for _ in range(10):
          newp = pos[i] + rng.integers(-rad, rad+1)
          if 0<=newp<n and newp not in others:
              newpos=pos.copy(); newpos[i]=newp
              if newpos.min()>0: newpos-=newpos.min()
              newn=newpos.max()+1
              # if newn>n maybe allow? It increases score; skip newn>n.
              if newn>n: continue
              pair_flat=(newpos[:,None]+newpos[None,:]).ravel()
              c=conv_weights(w,pair_flat,2*newn-1)
              sc=2*newn*c.max()
              if sc<best_score: update best_pos,best_n,best_score; pos=newpos;n=newn; break
    return best_pos,best_n,best_w,best_score
```
This recomputes pos min; if min>0 subtract. When shifting, the positions array changed; n may shrink. The weight vector unchanged. Good.
We should avoid spending too long; position moves O(m²) per trial. m=160, 25k; 1000 trials 25M, fine. We can allocate 20-30s.

After position moves, weights may become suboptimal; call weight optimize again.

```
def normalize_pos(pos):
   if len(pos)==0: return pos,1
   pos=pos.astype(np.int64)
   mn=int(pos.min())
   if mn>0: pos=pos-mn
   n=int(pos.max())+1
   return pos,n

def make_dense_sequence(w,pos,n):
   full=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
   full[pos]=w
   # if full.sum()<0.01: scale? w sum1 yes.
   return [float(x) for x in full]
```

def list_curve_params(p, rng, count=300):
    a_vals = [1,2,3,4,5, p-1,p-2,p-3,p-4]
    b_vals = [0,1,-1,2,-2,3,-3,4,-4]
    c0_vals = [0,1,2]
    c1_vals = [1,2,3,4,5, p-1,p-2,p-3, p//3, (2*p)//3]
    params=[]
    for c0 in c0_vals:
      for a1 in a_vals:
        for b1 in b_vals:
          for c1 in c1_vals:
             params.append((1,0,c0%p, a1%p,b1%p,c1%p))
             if len(params)>=count: return params
    # add random if not enough
    while len(params)<count:
       c0=int(rng.integers(p)); c1=int(rng.integers(1,p))
       a1=int(rng.integers(1,p)); b1=int(rng.integers(p))
       params.append((1,0,c0,a1%p,b1%p,c1%p))
    return params
```

def normalized_weights(w):
    s=float(w.sum())
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.ones(len(w))/len(w)
    w=np.asarray(w,float)
    w=np.maximum(w,0.0)
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: ...
    return w/s

`active_gradient_step(c,w,pair_matrix,Nbin,q,eta_list)`:
```
def active_gradient_step(c, w, pair_matrix, Nbin, q, eta_list):
    q=min(max(1,q), Nbin, len(c))
    top = np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
    p = np.zeros(Nbin, dtype=float)
    p[top]=1.0/len(top)
    B = p[pair_matrix]
    g = 2.0 * (B @ w)
    best_w=None; best_c=None; best_max=np.inf
    for eta in eta_list:
       w2=w*np.exp(-eta*g)
       s=w2.sum()
       if not finite or s<=0: continue
       w2/=s
       c2=conv_weights(w2, pair_matrix.ravel(), Nbin) # but pair_matrix.ravel would be view; okay
       max2=float(c2.max())
       if max2<best_max: best...
    return best_w,best_c,best_max
```
Need `pair_matrix` for B; in conv we use flat. Create pair_flat separately. Avoid `pair_matrix.ravel()` inside each step? Pass pair_flat. Simpler signature: `active_gradient_step(c,w,pair_matrix,pair_flat,Nbin,q,eta_list)`.

`optimize_weights_fixed(pos,n,w,rng,budget)` full:
Detailed implementation with time checks.
```
def optimize_weights_fixed(pos, n, w, rng, budget):
    pos=np.asarray(pos,int); n=int(n)
    m=len(pos)
    w=normalized_weights(w)
    Nbin=2*n-1
    pair_matrix=(pos[:,None]+pos[None,:])
    pair_flat=pair_matrix.ravel()
    pos_plus=[pos[i]+pos for i in range(m)]
    c=conv_weights(w,pair_flat,Nbin)
    cur_max=float(c.max())
    best_w=w.copy(); best_max=cur_max
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0.0,budget)
    # active gradient phase
    grad_deadline=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+max(1.0,budget*0.35))
    improved_any=True
    while time.monotonic()<grad_deadline:
       step_improved=False
       for q in [5,10,20,50,100]:
          bw,bc,bm=active_gradient_step(...)
          if bw is not None and bm < cur_max - 1e-15:
             w,c,cur_max = bw,bc,bm
             step_improved=True
             if cur_max<best_max: best... 
             break
       if not step_improved: break
    # local moves
    fracs=(0.02,0.05,0.08,0.12,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.75,0.95)
    no_improve=0; kick_every=100; temp=max(1e-12, cur_max*1e-4)
    best_no_improve_at = no_improve? 
    while time.monotonic()<deadline - 0.2:
       # choose donor/recipient
       # Use random but avoid zero weights
       while True:
          i=rng.integers(m)
          j=rng.integers(m)
          if i!=j and w[i]>0 and w[j]>=0: break
       # if want bias, perhaps use args: with prog 0.7 use i=choice(w), j=uniform
       # Let's implement simple uniform.
       donor=i; recipient=j
       a=float(w[donor])
       if a<=0: continue
       # candidate deltas
       candidate_delta=None; candidate_max=np.inf; candidate_c=None
       for frac in fracs:
           delta=frac*a
           if delta>=a: delta=0.999*a
           c2,maxc = trial_move_cand(c,w,pos_plus,pos,donor,recipient,delta)
           if maxc < candidate_max:
               candidate_max=maxc; candidate_delta=delta; candidate_c=c2
       # If best candidate improves current:
       if candidate_max < cur_max - 1e-15:
           c=candidate_c; w[donor]-=candidate_delta; w[recipient]+=candidate_delta; cur_max=candidate_max
           if cur_max<best_max:
              best_w=w.copy(); best_max=cur_max
              temp=max(1e-12, cur_max*1e-4)
           no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve % kick_every == 0 and candidate_max < cur_max + temp:
               # accept kick
               c=candidate_c; w[donor]-=candidate_delta; w[recipient]+=candidate_delta; cur_max=candidate_max
               if cur_max<best_max: best...
               temp*=0.5
              # if no candidate acceptable, do a random tiny move? 
       # occasionally re-normalize weights to fix small negative?
       if no_improve > 2000:
           # perturb: mix with lognormal or uniform
           w = 0.5*normalized_weights(w) + 0.5*normalized_weights(np.exp(rng.normal(0,1.0,m)))
           c=conv_weights(w,pair_flat,Nbin); cur_max=c.max(); no_improve=0
           if cur_max<best... 
       # time check
    return best_w,best_max
```

`process_support` as described:
```
def process_support(pos,n,w_init,rng,budget,seed):
    pos,n=normalize_pos(pos)
    w=normalized_weights(w_init)
    best_score=inf; best_pos=None; best_n=1; best_w=None
    # initial uniform and random maybe
    inits=[]
    inits.append(np.ones(m)/m)
    inits.append(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.7,m))/sum)
    # time per init
    for idx, wi in enumerate(inits):
       rem=budget-elapsed; if rem<5: break
       w_opt, maxc = optimize_weights_fixed(pos,n,wi,rng, rem*0.45)
       sc=2*n*maxc
       if sc<best_score: update best_pos=pos,best_n=n,best_w=w_opt,best_score=sc
       # position moves on best? Use w_opt
       pos2,n2,w2,sc2 = position_moves(pos,n,w_opt,rng, rem*0.15)
       if sc2<best_score: update
       # optimize weights after position
       w3,max3=optimize_weights_fixed(pos2,n2,w2,rng, rem*0.35)
       sc3=2*n2*max3
       if sc3<best_score: update
    return best_pos,best_n,best_w,best_score
```

`conv_weights`:
```
def conv_weights(w, pair_flat, Nbin):
    outer = (w[:, None] * w[None, :]).ravel()
    c = np.bincount(pair_flat, weights=outer, minlength=int(Nbin))
    if c.size < Nbin: c=np.concatenate((c, np.zeros(Nbin-c.size)))
    elif c.size > Nbin: c=c[:Nbin]
    c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
`pair_flat` could be view reshaped; ensure 1D int. `np.bincount` with float weights returns float64. If `Nbin` Python int.

`score_from_c(c,n)`: return 2*n*c.max() because sum=1.

```python
"""
Search for low-convolution nonnegative step functions.

Strategy
--------
Sparese weighted supports are much more competitive than dense uniform sequences.
This program constructs candidate supports from finite-field curves in a p x p
grid, then aggressively optimizes the nonnegative weights (and occasionally the
positions) so that the unnormalized convolution
max_k sum_{i+j=k} a_i a_j is as small as possible for sum(a)=1.

A finite-field curve pair yields roughly 2p support points in a length about p^2,
i.e. ratio n/m^2 close to 1/4.  With only a small constant of pairwise-sum
collisions, the weighted minimax objective often moves well below 2.  The code
then performs reusable local search:
  * active-set gradient-like multiplicative updates for the weights,
  * exact mass-moving coordinate tries using fast O(m) convolution updates,
  * position moves that may reduce the effective length,
  * a final refinement of the best found support.

The returned sequence is dense, nonnegative, finite, and usually has length
around 6000-9000, keeping exact evaluation feasible.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def normalize_pos(pos):
    """Shift integer positions to minimum 0 and return (pos, span_length)."""
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    if pos.size == 0:
        return pos, 1
    mn = int(pos.min())
    if mn != 0:
        pos = pos - mn
    n = int(pos.max()) + 1
    if n < 1:
        n = 1
    return pos, n


def normalized_weights(w):
    """Return nonnegative float weights normalized to sum 1."""
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
    w = np.maximum(w, 0.0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not math.isfinite(s):
        w = np.ones(w.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(w.size)
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(w.size, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, w.size)
    w = w / s
    w = np.maximum(w, 0.0)
    s2 = float(w.sum())
    if s2 > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s2):
        w = w / s2
    return w


def prime_sieve(limit):
    """Simple prime sieve for small limits."""
    if limit < 2:
        return []
    sieve = [True] * (limit + 1)
    sieve[0] = False
    sieve[1] = False
    r = int(math.isqrt(limit))
    for i in range(2, r + 1):
        if sieve[i]:
            step = i
            start = i * i
            sieve[start::step] = [False] * (((limit - start) // step) + 1)
    out = []
    for i in range(2, limit + 1):
        if sieve[i]:
            out.append(i)
    return out


def make_dense_sequence(w, pos, n):
    """Make dense list of length n with weights pos applied."""
    out = np.zeros(int(max(1, n)), dtype=np.float64)
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    ok = (pos >= 0) & (pos < out.size) & np.isfinite(w)
    if np.any(ok):
        out[pos[ok]] = w[ok]
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        out = out * 100.0
    return [float(x) for x in out]


def conv_weights(w, pair_sums_flat, n_bins):
    """
    Exact convolution coefficient array for weights on support positions.

    pair_sums_flat contains pos_i + pos_j for all ordered pairs (i,j).
    Returns float64 array of length at least n_bins.
    """
    w = normalized_weights(w)
    outer = (w[:, None] * w[None, :]).ravel()
    c = np.bincount(pair_sums_flat, weights=outer, minlength=int(n_bins))
    if c.size < n_bins:
        c = np.concatenate((c, np.zeros(int(n_bins) - c.size, dtype=np.float64)))
    else:
        c = c[:int(n_bins)]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def support_initial_score(pos, n):
    """Unweighted equal-weight score for a support, for ranking candidates."""
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    m = int(pos.size)
    if m < 2:
        return float("inf")
    n = int(n)
    N = 2 * n - 1
    pair = (pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]).ravel()
    counts = np.bincount(pair, minlength=N)
    rmax = int(counts.max())
    return 2.0 * n * rmax / float(m * m)


def candidate_sort_key(item):
    return item[0]


def generate_candidates_for_prime(p, rng, max_params=220, max_output=12, max_n=9500):
    """Generate and rank curve-pair supports for a prime p."""
    candidates = []
    i = np.arange(p, dtype=np.int64)

    a_vals = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, p - 1, p - 2, p - 3, p - 4]
    b_vals = [0, 1, -1, 2, -2, 3, -3, 4, -4]
    c0_vals = [0, 1, 2, max(1, p // 3), max(1, (2 * p) // 3)]
    c1_vals = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, p - 1, p - 2, p - 3, max(1, p // 3), max(1, (2 * p) // 3)]

    params = []
    for c0 in c0_vals:
        for a1 in a_vals:
            for b1 in b_vals:
                for c1 in c1_vals:
                    params.append((1, 0, c0 % p, a1 % p, b1 % p, c1 % p))
                    if len(params) >= max_params:
                        break
                if len(params) >= max_params:
                    break
            if len(params) >= max_params:
                break
        if len(params) >= max_params:
            break

    # Add some random algebraic curves if the deterministic list was short.
    while len(params) < max_params:
        c0 = int(rng.integers(p))
        a1 = int(rng.integers(p))
        b1 = int(rng.integers(p))
        c1 = int(rng.integers(p))
        params.append((1, 0, c0 % p, a1 % p, b1 % p, c1 % p))
        if len(params) > 3 * max_params:
            break

    for (a0, b0, c0, a1, b1, c1) in params:
        y0 = (a0 * i * i + b0 * i + c0) % p
        y1 = (a1 * i * i + b1 * i + c1) % p
        p0 = i * p + y0
        p1 = i * p + y1
        seen = set()
        for v in p0:
            seen.add(int(v))
        for v in p1:
            seen.add(int(v))
        if len(seen) < int(1.7 * p):
            continue
        pos = np.array(sorted(seen), dtype=np.int64)
        pos, n = normalize_pos(pos)
        if n > max_n or n < 10:
            continue
        sc = support_initial_score(pos, n)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((sc, pos.copy()))
        if len(candidates) >= 400:
            break

    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    out = []
    seen_shapes = set()
    for sc, cand_pos in candidates[:max_output * 4]:
        key = tuple(cand_pos.tolist())
        if key in seen_shapes:
            continue
        seen_shapes.add(key)
        n = int(cand_pos.max()) + 1
        out.append((sc, len(out) + 1, cand_pos, n))
        if len(out) >= max_output:
            break
    return out


def active_gradient_step(c, w, pair_matrix, pair_flat, n_bins, max_c, q, eta_list):
    """
    One smooth active-set step for min sum_k p_k c_k.
    Returns (best_w, best_c, best_max) if an improving candidate is found.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = normalized_weights(w)
    m = int(w.size)
    q = int(min(max(1, q), c.size))
    if q <= 1 and c.size > 1:
        q = 2
    if max_c <= 1e-300:
        return None, None, float("inf")

    top = np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
    top = top[np.isfinite(c[top]) & (c[top] >= 0.0)]
    if top.size == 0:
        return None, None, float("inf")

    thr = 0.92 * max_c
    vals = c[top] - thr
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-300)
    s = float(vals.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        vals = np.ones(vals.size, dtype=np.float64) / vals.size
    else:
        vals = vals / s

    p = np.zeros(int(n_bins), dtype=np.float64)
    p[top] = vals
    B = p[pair_matrix]
    g = 2.0 * (B.dot(w))
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)

    g0 = float(g.min())
    best_w = None
    best_c = None
    best_max = float("inf")

    for eta in eta_list:
        w2 = w * np.exp(-float(eta) * (g - g0))
        s = float(w2.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
            continue
        w2 = w2 / s
        c2 = conv_weights(w2, pair_flat, n_bins)
        mx = float(c2.max())
        if math.isfinite(mx) and mx < best_max:
            best_max = mx
            best_w = w2
            best_c = c2
    return best_w, best_c, best_max


def trial_move_cand(c, w, pos_plus, pos, donor, recipient, delta):
    """
    Evaluate moving mass delta from support point donor to recipient.
    Uses exact O(m) update of the convolution array.
    """
    c2 = c.copy()
    coef = 2.0 * delta
    pd = pos_plus[donor]
    pr = pos_plus[recipient]
    c2[pd] -= coef * w
    c2[pr] += coef * w

    d2 = delta * delta
    sd = int(2 * pos[donor])
    sr = int(2 * pos[recipient])
    sj = int(pos[donor] + pos[recipient])
    c2[sd] += d2
    c2[sj] -= 2.0 * d2
    c2[sr] += d2

    mx = float(c2.max())
    return c2, mx


def optimize_weights_fixed(pos, n, w, rng, budget):
    """
    Optimize weights for a fixed support and length.
    Returns (best_weights, best_max_convolution).
    """
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    m = int(pos.size)
    if m < 2:
        return normalized_weights(w), 0.0

    w = normalized_weights(w)
    n_bins = 2 * n - 1
    pair_matrix = pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]
    pair_flat = pair_matrix.ravel()
    pos_plus = [pos[i] + pos for i in range(m)]

    c = conv_weights(w, pair_flat, n_bins)
    cur_max = float(c.max())
    best_w = w.copy()
    best_max = cur_max

    if budget <= 0.0:
        return best_w, best_max

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget))

    eta_list = (0.05, 0.10, 0.18, 0.30, 0.55, 0.90, 1.50)
    grad_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(0.4, 0.32 * budget))

    # Phase 1: active-set gradient-like multiplicative updates.
    while time.monotonic() < grad_deadline:
        step = None
        step_max = float("inf")
        for q in (5, 10, 20, 40, 80):
            bw, bc, bm = active_gradient_step(c, w, pair_matrix, pair_flat, n_bins, cur_max, q, eta_list)
            if bw is not None and bm < step_max - 1e-15:
                step = (bw, bc, bm)
                step_max = bm
        if step is None:
            break
        w, c, cur_max = step
        if cur_max < best_max:
            best_max = cur_max
            best_w = w.copy()

    # Phase 2: exact local mass-moving search.
    fracs = (0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.10, 0.18, 0.30, 0.50, 0.75, 0.95)
    no_improve = 0
    kick_every = 220
    temp = max(1e-12, cur_max * 1e-4)
    trials = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
        # Choose donor/recipient.  Bias high -> low occasionally.
        roll = rng.random()
        if roll < 0.62:
            pw = w / max(1e-300, float(w.sum()))
            donor = int(rng.choice(m, p=pw))
            qlow = 1.0 / (w + 1e-12)
            qlow /= float(qlow.sum())
            recipient = int(rng.choice(m, p=qlow))
        elif roll < 0.85:
            donor = int(rng.integers(m))
            recipient = int(rng.integers(m))
        else:
            oi = np.argsort(w)
            donor = int(oi[0])
            recipient = int(oi[m - 1])

        if donor == recipient:
            recipient = (donor + 1) % m
        a = float(w[donor])
        if a <= 0.0:
            continue

        cand_c = None
        cand_max = float("inf")
        cand_delta = 0.0
        for fr in fracs:
            delta = fr * a
            if delta >= a:
                delta = 0.999 * a
            if delta <= 0.0:
                continue
            c2, mx = trial_move_cand(c, w, pos_plus, pos, donor, recipient, delta)
            if not math.isfinite(mx):
                continue
            if mx < cand_max:
                cand_max = mx
                cand_c = c2
                cand_delta = delta

        if cand_c is None:
            continue

        if cand_max < cur_max - 1e-15:
            c = cand_c
            w[donor] -= cand_delta
            w[recipient] += cand_delta
            cur_max = cand_max
            no_improve = 0
            temp = max(1e-12, cur_max * 1e-4)
            if cur_max < best_max:
                best_max = cur_max
                best_w = w.copy()
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % kick_every == 0:
                if cand_max < cur_max + temp and cand_max < cur_max * 1.5:
                    c = cand_c
                    w[donor] -= cand_delta
                    w[recipient] += cand_delta
                    cur_max = cand_max
                    temp = max(1e-12, temp * 0.55)
                    if cur_max < best_max:
                        best_max = cur_max
                        best_w = w.copy()
                else:
                    temp = max(1e-12, temp * 0.7)

            if no_improve > 3500:
                # Restart around current best with noise, but keep best.
                old = w
                rand = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, m))
                w = 0.55 * old + 0.45 * normalized_weights(rand)
                w = normalized_weights(w)
                c = conv_weights(w, pair_flat, n_bins)
                cur_max = float(c.max())
                no_improve = 0

        trials += 1
        if trials % 80 == 0 and time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

    w = normalized_weights(w)
    # Re-normalize and final exact check.
    c = conv_weights(w, pair_flat, n_bins)
    mx = float(c.max())
    if mx < best_max:
        best_max = mx
        best_w = w.copy()
    return best_w, best_max


def position_moves(pos, n, w, rng, budget):
    """
    Local search over support positions with fixed weights.
    Returns updated (pos, n, weights, score).
    """
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel().copy()
    n = int(n)
    w = normalized_weights(w)
    m = int(pos.size)
    if m < 2 or budget <= 0.0:
        return pos, n, w, 2.0 * n * float(conv_weights(w, (pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]).ravel(), 2 * n - 1).max())

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget))

    pair = (pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]).ravel()
    N = 2 * n - 1
    c = conv_weights(w, pair, N)
    cur_score = 2.0 * n * float(c.max())

    best_pos = pos.copy()
    best_n = n
    best_w = w.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    pos_set = set(pos.tolist())

    attempts = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
        iu = int(rng.integers(m))
        rad = int(max(1, 1 + n * (0.01 + 0.14 * rng.random())))
        for _ in range(18):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            off = int(rng.integers(-rad, rad + 1))
            np1 = int(pos[iu] + off)
            if np1 < 0 or np1 >= n:
                continue
            if np1 != int(pos[iu]) and np1 in pos_set:
                continue

            newpos = pos.copy()
            newpos[iu] = np1
            if newpos.min() > 0:
                newpos -= newpos.min()
            newn = int(newpos.max()) + 1
            if newn > n:
                continue

            newpair = (newpos[:, None] + newpos[None, :]).ravel()
            nc = conv_weights(w, newpair, 2 * newn - 1)
            nscore = 2.0 * newn * float(nc.max())
            if nscore < best_score - 1e-15:
                best_pos = newpos.copy()
                best_n = newn
                best_w = w.copy()
                best_score = nscore
                pos = newpos
                n = newn
                pair = newpair
                N = 2 * n - 1
                c = nc
                curr_score = nscore
                pos_set = set(pos.tolist())
                break

        attempts += 1
        if attempts % 60 == 0 and time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

    return best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score


def process_support(pos, n, w_init, rng, budget):
    """
    Refine a support by optimizing weights, moving positions, and repeating.
    Returns (pos, n, weights, score) or None if invalid.
    """
    if budget <= 0.2:
        return None
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if pos.size < 2:
        return None
    pos, n = normalize_pos(pos)
    if pos.size < 2:
        return None

    m = int(pos.size)
    if w_init is None:
        w0 = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m
    else:
        w0 = normalized_weights(np.asarray(w_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel())

    pair0 = (pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]).ravel()
    c0 = conv_weights(w0, pair0, 2 * n - 1)
    s0 = 2.0 * n * float(c0.max())

    best_pos = pos.copy()
    best_n = n
    best_w = w0.copy()
    best_score = s0

    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(0.0, float(budget))
    hard_limit = start + total_budget

    # Phase 1: optimize initial weights.
    b1 = max(1.0, 0.42 * total_budget)
    if time.monotonic() < hard_limit - 1.0:
        w1, mx1 = optimize_weights_fixed(pos, n, w0, rng, min(b1, hard_limit - time.monotonic()))
        sc1 = 2.0 * n * float(mx1)
        if sc1 < best_score:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = pos.copy(), n, w1.copy(), sc1
        w = w1
    else:
        w = w0

    # Phase 2: move positions.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    b2 = max(0.4, 0.10 * total_budget)
    if time.monotonic() < hard_limit - 1.0:
        p2, n2, w2, sc2 = position_moves(pos, n, w, rng, min(b2, max(0.0, hard_limit - time.monotonic())))
        if sc2 < best_score:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = p2.copy(), n2, w2.copy(), sc2
        pos, n, w = p2, n2, w2

    # Phase 3: re-optimize weights at moved positions.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    b3 = max(1.0, 0.30 * total_budget)
    if time.monotonic() < hard_limit - 1.0:
        w3, mx3 = optimize_weights_fixed(pos, n, w, rng, min(b3, max(0.0, hard_limit - time.monotonic())))
        sc3 = 2.0 * n * float(mx3)
        if sc3 < best_score:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = pos.copy(), n, w3.copy(), sc3
        w = w3

    # Phase 4: move positions again.
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
    b4 = max(0.2, 0.06 * total_budget)
    if time.monotonic() < hard_limit - 1.0:
        p4, n4, w4, sc4 = position_moves(pos, n, w, rng, min(b4, max(0.0, hard_limit - time.monotonic())))
        if sc4 < best_score:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = p4.copy(), n4, w4.copy(), sc4
        pos, n, w = p4, n4, w4

    # Phase 5: use remaining time for a random restart on the best current support.
    rem = max(0.0, hard_limit - time.monotonic())
    if rem > 2.0:
        rw = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.8, best_pos.size))
        rw = normalized_weights(rw)
        rw2, mxr = optimize_weights_fixed(best_pos, best_n, rw, rng, min(0.5 * rem, rem - 0.5))
        scr = 2.0 * best_n * float(mxr)
        if scr < best_score:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = best_pos.copy(), best_n, rw2.copy(), scr
        rem2 = max(0.0, hard_limit - time.monotonic())
        if rem2 > 1.5:
            p5, n5, w5, sc5 = position_moves(best_pos, best_n, best_w, rng, min(0.3 * rem2, rem2 - 0.5))
            if sc5 < best_score:
                best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = p5.copy(), n5, w5.copy(), sc5

    # Final trim of negligible weights if it improves score.
    for _ in range(25):
        keep = best_w > 1e-12
        if keep.all():
            break
        pnew = best_pos[keep]
        wnew = best_w[keep]
        pnew, nnew = normalize_pos(pnew)
        if pnew.size < 2:
            break
        wnew = normalized_weights(wnew)
        pairnew = (pnew[:, None] + pnew[None, :]).ravel()
        cnew = conv_weights(wnew, pairnew, 2 * nnew - 1)
        snew = 2.0 * nnew * float(cnew.max())
        if snew < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score = pnew, nnew, wnew, snew
        else:
            break

    best_w = normalized_weights(best_w)
    pairf = (best_pos[:, None] + best_pos[None, :]).ravel()
    cfin = conv_weights(best_w, pairf, 2 * best_n - 1)
    sfin = 2.0 * best_n * float(cfin.max())
    if sfin < best_score:
        best_score = sfin
    return best_pos.copy(), best_n, best_w.copy(), float(best_score)


def fallback_sequence():
    """Safe valid fallback if the search produces nothing."""
    try:
        par = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if isinstance(par, list) and len(par) > 0:
            vals = []
            ok = True
            for x in par:
                if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)):
                    ok = False
                    break
                if math.isnan(float(x)) or math.isinf(float(x)):
                    ok = False
                    break
                vals.append(max(0.0, min(1000.0, float(x))))
            if ok and math.fsum(vals) >= 0.01:
                return vals
    except Exception:
        pass
    return [1.0] * 256


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Entry point required by the harness.
    """
    start_total = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    hard_deadline = start_total + total_budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_pos = None
    best_n = 1
    best_w = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def current_remaining():
        return max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic())

    # Generate candidate supports from finite-field curves.
    primes = [p for p in prime_sieve(100) if 31 <= p <= 97]
    cands = []
    counter = 0
    gen_deadline = min(start_total + 70.0, hard_deadline - 200.0)

    for p in primes:
        if time.monotonic() > gen_deadline:
            break
        got = generate_candidates_for_prime(p, rng, max_params=180, max_output=8, max_n=9500)
        for sc, pos, n in got:
            counter += 1
            cands.append((sc, counter, pos, n))
        print(f"[support generation] p={p}, candidates so far={len(cands)}")

    if not cands:
        # Fallback: build one deterministic support for p=83 if still possible.
        try:
            got = generate_candidates_for_prime(83, rng, max_params=50, max_output=3, max_n=9000)
            for sc, pos, n in got:
                counter += 1
                cands.append((sc, counter, pos, n))
        except Exception:
            pass

    if not cands:
        return fallback_sequence()

    cands.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)
    print(f"[candidates] ready={len(cands)}, best unweighted={cands[0][0]:.6f}")

    def update_best(pos, n, w, sc):
        nonlocal best_pos, best_n, best_w, best_score
        if pos is None or w is None:
            return
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            return
        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int64).copy()
            best_n = int(n)
            best_w = normalized_weights(w).copy()
            best_score = float(sc)
            print(f"[new best] score={best_score:.8f} n={best_n} support={best_pos.size}")

    # Short probes on promising candidates.
    probed = []
    probe_count = min(16, len(cands))
    for idx in range(probe_count):
        if current_remaining() < 60.0:
            break
        sc0, cnt, pos, n = cands[idx]
        alloc = min(7.0, max(1.0, current_remaining() / max(1, (probe_count - idx))))
        res = process_support(pos, n, None, rng, alloc)
        if res is not None:
            p, nn, ww, ss = res
            update_best(p, nn, ww, ss)
            probed.append((ss, idx, p, nn, ww))
    if probed:
        probed.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)
        print(f"[probe best] score={probed[0][0]:.8f}")

    # Deep processing on best probed supports and top unweighted supports.
    deep_list = []
    seen_idx = set()
    for item in probed[:2]:
        _, idx, p, nn, ww = item
        if idx not in seen_idx:
            seen_idx.add(idx)
            deep_list.append((p, nn, ww))
    for idx in range(min(5, len(cands))):
        if len(deep_list) >= 4:
            break
        if idx in seen_idx:
            continue
        sc0, cnt, pos, n = cands[idx]
        deep_list.append((pos, n, None))
        seen_idx.add(idx)

    deep_iter = 0
    for p, nn, ww in deep_list:
        if current_remaining() < 120.0:
            break
        alloc = min(220.0, max(20.0, current_remaining() / max(1, (len(deep_list) - deep_iter))))
        res = process_support(p, nn, ww, rng, alloc)
        if res is not None:
            ps, ns, ws, ss = res
            update_best(ps, ns, ws, ss)
        deep_iter += 1

    # Final long refinement of the best object.
    if best_pos is not None:
        rem = current_remaining()
        if rem > 60.0:
            res = process_support(best_pos, best_n, best_w, rng, rem - 5.0)
            if res is not None:
                ps, ns, ws, ss = res
                update_best(ps, ns, ws, ss)

    if best_pos is None:
        # Last-resort simple support or parent.
        try:
            sc0, cnt, pos, n = cands[0]
            m = pos.size
            w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m
            pair = (pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]).ravel()
            c = conv_weights(w, pair, 2 * n - 1)
            s = 2.0 * n * float(c.max())
            update_best(pos, n, w, s)
        except Exception:
            pass

    if best_pos is None:
        return fallback_sequence()

    # Final validation / trimming.
    best_pos, best_n = normalize_pos(best_pos)
    best_w = normalized_weights(best_w)
    pair = (best_pos[:, None] + best_pos[None, :]).ravel()
    c = conv_weights(best_w, pair, 2 * best_n - 1)
    final_score = 2.0 * best_n * float(c.max())
    update_best(best_pos, best_n, best_w, final_score)

    seq = make_dense_sequence(best_w, best_pos, best_n)

    # Ensure the evaluator-facing sum is safely above threshold.
    ssum = float(sum(seq))
    if ssum < 0.01:
        seq = [x * 1000.0 for x in seq]

    # Clip to evaluator bounds, just in case.
    final_seq = []
    for x in seq:
        if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)):
            continue
        xx = float(x)
        if math.isnan(xx) or math.isinf(xx):
            continue
        xx = max(0.0, xx)
        xx = min(1000.0, xx)
        final_seq.append(xx)

    if len(final_seq) == 0:
        final_seq = fallback_sequence()

    print(f"[final] n={len(final_seq)} estimated_score={best_score:.10f}")
    return final_seq
```
